# Spanish 03 — B1 independent communication

Conceptual deck: `deck:es-MX:03`. This text source contains exactly 1,000 cards,
`es-03-0001`–`es-03-1000`, in learning order. Production follows educated
contemporary central Mexican Spanish with appropriate tú, usted and ustedes.
Each card has one primary actual hidden learning point. Incidental visible forms
are support, not additional retrieved targets. Card count does not measure exact
word knowledge, distinct senses, mastery, or examination readiness.

The execution is [issue #15](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/issues/15),
under [roadmap #1](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/issues/1) and
[Spanish plan #2](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/issues/2). Allocations are
ORCHESTRATOR authoring decisions within the
[pinned guide](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/blob/e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f/languages/es.md),
not official DELE/SIELE inventories. Accepted prerequisites are Spanish 01/600
and 02/800 at `5ab6c12dcea6a4e15e5e4e6b84ab5a6f6e5204e4`. All guide links below
pin `e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f`. Earlier records are prerequisite
evidence, not targets of a renewed audit. Editorial acceptance below is separate
from ORCHESTRATOR acceptance: the accepted Spanish total remains 1,400 until this
stage is reviewed and accepted, at which point the cumulative total is 2,400.

Only logical `audio/<id>.mp3` references are supplied. No recordings, listening,
native-human review, runtime preparation, export or application behavior are
claimed. Past subjunctive remains allocated to stage 04; present-stage hypotheses
use supported noun/infinitive frames or current open conditions.

## Fixed allocation and prerequisite chain

| Block / objective | IDs | Cards | Actual hidden objectives | Prerequisite basis |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| A / es-03-A | 0001–0150 | 150 | Connected narrative event/background/repetition/duration, narrative lexical senses, attributed Spain perfect recognition. | Accepted 02 past/time and explicit introductory support. |
| B / es-03-B | 0151–0290 | 140 | Future/conditional prediction, hypothesis and stance; present subjunctive wish/request/evaluation and contextual assertion contrasts. | 01 modals, 02 event frames, A. |
| C / es-03-C | 0291–0430 | 140 | Commands with complete attached forms, direct/indirect order and attachment restrictions, recoverable participants and actual grammatical choices. | Accepted 02 objects/commands, A–B. |
| D / es-03-D | 0431–0560 | 130 | Passive and impersonal se, affected-person accidents and contrasts with other known se roles. | Accepted 02 bounded se, C participant tracking. |
| E / es-03-E | 0561–0680 | 120 | Relative/reporting frames, cause/purpose/result/opposition, current si conditions and attributed source stance. | Accepted 02 relatives, A–D. |
| F / es-03-F | 0681–0780 | 100 | Interpretation-changing ser/estar adjective senses and systematic por/para relations. | Accepted 01–02 bases, B mood. |
| G / es-03-G | 0781–0900 | 120 | Periphrases and actual cuenta/llevar senses, complements, collocations and communicative extensions. | Accepted 02 duration/periphrases, A–F. |
| H / es-03-H | 0901–1000 | 100 | Ordinary/social lexical breadth, repair/explanation and attributed Spain/Colombia/River Plate recognition with Mexican-compatible replies or paraphrases. | A–G and introduced local vocabulary/address support. |
| Total | 0001–1000 | 1000 | Complete assigned §6.4 inventory and applicable cross-cutting requirements. | Actual valid earlier IDs or explicit introductory support in every mapping. |

The actual role split is 950 P / 50 R: Madrid recognition in 0131–0140 and regional recognition in 0961–1000.

P denotes Mexican-compatible production. R denotes explicitly attributed
recognition in A/H, not a different study mode. Regional names identify the actual
speaker/context, not exclusivity of a shared expression. Familiar vos/vosotros
are attributed recognition only. Written pronunciation explanation supplies no
listening evidence.

## Enumerated objectives and batch review

Before each batch, 40 concrete senses, complements, collocations, grammatical
choices and functions were enumerated within its fixed IDs. Authors and editors
received the actual accepted target/sense inventory and relevant complete earlier
cards. A changed name, noun, person or tense alone was not credited as novelty;
where that was the only difference, the compact hidden component was repaired
within the original allocated objective. Each mapping records the final actual
hidden point and distinguishes introduced support from earlier retrieved learning.

| Batch | IDs | Objectives and meaningful contrasts |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 01 | 0001–0040 | Past ongoing states versus event changes, successful versus unused opportunities, recollection, narrative change and actual lexical extensions. |
| 02 | 0041–0080 | Completed duration versus repeated background, onset/change, persistence, interrupted activity and connected narrative vocabulary. |
| 03 | 0081–0120 | Ordinary travel/work/household incidents, event/background timing, narrative causes and practical consequences. |
| 04 | 0121–0160 | Narrative evidence and change, locally attributed Madrid perfect uses and new narrative senses, Mexican explanations and initial future predictions/commitments. |
| 05 | 0161–0200 | Future irregular forms, present conjecture, deferred decisions, anticipation/concession and promises; first conditional choices, advice, qualified opinion and future relative to a past report. |
| 06 | 0201–0240 | Conditional advice and tradeoffs, past conjecture, reported expectations; present wishes, requests, necessity/evaluation, doubt and asserted information. |
| 07 | 0241–0280 | Indicative indirect uncertainty, factive emotional subjunctive, pending versus habitual temporal relations, concession, desired versus known referents and directive versus reporting predicates. |
| 08 | 0281–0320 | Need/sufficiency, fear/confidence, prevention/causation and current suggestions/reminders; affirmative commands with explicit direct/indirect objects and beneficiaries. |
| 09 | 0321–0360 | Negative commands and complete clitic blocks, affected/ethical/possessive participants, recipient versus reflexive interpretations. |
| 10 | 0361–0400 | Restricted clitic climbing versus required infinitive attachment, prepositional and subject infinitives, gerund attachment and expanded dative frames. |
| 11 | 0401–0440 | Respectful singular/plural commands, speaker-inclusive exhortations and attached spelling; initial passive se with patient agreement and new predicate senses. |
| 12 | 0441–0480 | Passive patient/number contrasts and lexical frames; impersonal se with unnamed human agents and singular agreement. |
| 13 | 0481–0520 | Impersonal human objects versus passive subjects, personal a and participant roles; affected-person mishaps and recoverable possessions. |
| 14 | 0521–0560 | Accidental/affected-person constructions, causal responsibility versus grammatical framing, and distinctions among passive, impersonal, pronominal and other se. |
| 15 | 0561–0600 | Relative complements, free/partitive/source/participant distinctions and genuine lexical extensions; initial reported position and source attribution. |
| 16 | 0601–0640 | Reported assertion, deixis and stance; cause, intended purpose and current conditions with concrete practical consequences. |
| 17 | 0641–0680 | Consequence/opposition, concession and reasoned repairs; open si conditions, contingency and conditional locutions in present plans. |
| 18 | 0681–0720 | Systematic interpretation-changing adjective senses in ser/estar descriptions, evaluations and visible changes. |
| 19 | 0721–0760 | Further copular interpretations; representation, sufficiency, presumed state, internal speech, budgeted purpose, duration, route, stance and pending/readiness relations. |
| 20 | 0761–0800 | Further por/para complements, result and discourse functions; modal, onset, transition, outcome, development and result frames, with new compact lexical components where earlier basics already exist. |
| 21 | 0801–0840 | Further frequent periphrases; realization, explicit de que complement, consideration and independent action in cuenta families. |
| 22 | 0841–0880 | Worthwhile return and further cuenta senses/complements; llevar duration-gerund, relationships and carrying out. |
| 23 | 0881–0920 | Further llevar collocations, clothing and related distinct senses; ordinary/social explanatory vocabulary. |
| 24 | 0921–0960 | Friendship, household, social plans, repair, clarification and broader everyday lexical senses. |
| 25 | 0961–1000 | Attributed Madrid vocabulary/address/discourse and written phonological explanation; Colombian local vocabulary/address; Buenos Aires voseo, vocabulary and discourse with Mexican-compatible responses. |

Exactly 25 fresh 40-card authors and 25 separate editors have completed the
sequential batch process. Every call used the required authorized Codex CLI
`gpt-6.1-sol`, high reasoning, ephemeral session, read-only sandbox and structured
JSON output. Unchanged CLI 0.163.0/model-access evidence from accepted
[PR #12](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/pull/12) and
[PR #14](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/pull/14) is reused. The executor read
the complete author outputs, concerns, editor findings and complete replacements,
then applied verified corrections and cheap affected-record/mapping comparisons.
No model fallback, installation, audio or later-stage work was used.

The one complete final editorial pass is complete in consecutive partitions
0001–0400, 0401–0800 and 0801–1000, using three further fresh auditors with the
same required settings. The executor inspected every complete output. The first
partition supplied five verified replacements: 0006 clarifies an explicitly
mistaken interpretation, 0038 corrects window-vocabulary support, 0057 and 0395
distinguish earlier stem evidence from introduced person forms, and 0365 removes
an unsupported earlier-trip presupposition. The other two partitions returned no
findings. One question about incidental room/paint/wet-paint support in
0026/0165/0214 was resolved by reading accepted 02-0475 in full; its sentence and
explicit introductory support establish all three claims. Verified final
corrections were applied together and affected mappings inspected.

Actual process totals are 25 authors, 25 separate editors and 3 final auditors:
53 successful content turns in distinct fresh sessions, from 54 CLI invocations.
One extra final-1 invocation was rejected before any model turn or review because
1,107,836 input characters exceeded the 1,048,576-character transport limit.
Removing duplicate compact entries for records already supplied in full retained
all cards, fields and inventory evidence; the completed review was not repeated.
No final editorial requirement remains unresolved. Per-card accepted/none records
completed editorial review; ORCHESTRATOR acceptance remains separate.

## Primary-reference decisions

Verified accepted 01/02 decisions are reused where the question is unchanged,
including Mexican past distribution, clitic placement/agreement and the accepted
feminine direct-object la in avisar a alguien de algo (02-0750). These decisions
were not reopened without a new question. Examples in all new cards are authored;
reference examples are not copied.

[RAE/ASALE Crónica 2023–2024, p.72](https://www.rae.es/sites/default/files/2024-09/Cronica%20de%20la%20lengua%20espanola%202023_2024_web.pdf#page=92) and NGLE [23.7](https://www.rae.es/gramática/sintaxis/el-pretérito-perfecto-compuesto-he-cantado-i-relevancia-actual-de-los-hechos-pretéritos)/[23.8](https://www.rae.es/gramática/sintaxis/el-pretérito-perfecto-compuesto-he-cantado-ii-el-perfecto-de-hechos-recientes-otros-usos) support context-led Mexican simple/compound past distribution and the locally attributed Madrid recent-event perfect. Continued/repeated experience and appropriate result readings remain available in Mexican production. Neither hoy nor a national label mechanically selects a tense. NGLE [28.7](https://www.rae.es/gramática/sintaxis/perífrasis-de-infinitivo-ii-negación-y-tiempo-en-las-perífrasis-modales-de-infinitivo) permits contextually unrealized past opportunities with poder; completed achievement is not entailed by every affirmative preterite.

NGLE [23.14](https://www.rae.es/gramática/sintaxis/el-futuro-simple-cantaré-futuros-sintéticos-y-analíticos)/[23.15](https://www.rae.es/gramática/sintaxis/el-condicional-simple-cantaría) distinguish future prediction/commitment, current conjecture/concession, conditional hypothesis and conjecture about a past state, and future relative to a past report. Current open si conditions and supported noun/infinitive hypotheses keep productive past subjunctive in stage 04; this allocation does not make other Spanish constructions ungrammatical.

NGLE [25.3](https://www.rae.es/gramática/sintaxis/el-modo-en-las-subordinadas-sustantivas-i-contextos-básicos-de-selección-del-modo), [25.9](https://www.rae.es/gram%C3%A1tica/sintaxis/el-modo-en-las-subordinadas-relativas-i-caracter%C3%ADsticas-generales), and [25.13–14](https://www.rae.es/gram%C3%A1tica/sintaxis/elecci%C3%B3n-del-modo-con-las-conjunciones-subordinantes) support actual assertion, influence, evaluation, desired referents and prospective temporal relations. Factive emotional subjunctive can presuppose truth; factual background with aunque can use subjunctive. [Doubt adverbs](https://www.rae.es/gram%C3%A1tica/sintaxis/elecci%C3%B3n-del-modo-con-preposiciones-adverbios-e-interjecciones) do not mechanically select one mood. The few accepted alternatives are verified for their particular visible contexts, not automatic synonym or mood lists. DPD [confiar](https://www.rae.es/dpd/confiar)/[miedo](https://www.rae.es/dpd/miedo) retain their required prepositions before que. The selected [recordar](https://www.rae.es/dpd/recordar) reminder frame does not imply a blanket subjunctive rule for recordar.

DPD [pronombres personales átonos](https://www.rae.es/dpd/pronombres%20personales%20%C3%A1tonos) supports attachment, se-first ordering, second-before-first-before-third person, whole-block placement and accentuation. First-plural commands lose s before nos; adjacent written s before se is not doubled; command-final n plus nos retains nn. NGLE [16.12](https://www.rae.es/gram%C3%A1tica/sintaxis/alternancias-en-la-posici%C3%B3n-de-los-pronombres-%C3%A1tonos)/[16.13](https://www.rae.es/gram%C3%A1tica/sintaxis/posici%C3%B3n-de-los-pronombres-%C3%A1tonos-en-estructuras-complejas) qualify climbing by actual construction: querer permits it, while selected insistir en/dedicarse a frames, hay que and infinitives functioning as subjects block it. Other prepositional/desire frames are not categorically banned. Selected canonical object agreement does not deny the documented educated-Mexican recipient-plural transfer. DPD [leísmo §4(a)](https://www.rae.es/dpd/le%C3%ADsmo) supports the selected nonagent clausal-stimulus dative experiencer with molestar; the same verb does not have an invariant case analysis in every construction.

DPD [se](https://www.rae.es/dpd/se) distinguishes patient-subject passive agreement, singular impersonal agency, reflexive/reciprocal and lexical-pronominal uses, and le/les replacement. Personal a does not by itself identify an indirect object, and bare indefinite human subjects can appear in passive constructions. Affected-person event framing does not remove causal responsibility. Accepted [quien](https://www.rae.es/dpd/quien)/[donde](https://www.rae.es/dpd/donde)/[adonde](https://www.rae.es/dpd/adonde) decisions supply bounded relative antecedent/complement support; new hidden learning is identified separately per row.

DPD [estar](https://www.rae.es/dpd/estar)/[ser](https://www.rae.es/dpd/ser) and NGLE [37.9](https://www.rae.es/gramática/sintaxis/el-atributo-en-las-oraciones-copulativas-con-ser-y-estar-iii-aspectos-sintácticos-y-léxicos) distinguish actual adjective interpretations, speaker construal and result states. A permanent/temporary slogan is insufficient; episodic behavior can occur with ser. Estar para and estar por have distinct readiness, pending-action or inclination readings in their actual contexts.

DEM [cuenta](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/cuenta)/[llevar](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/llevar) and DLE [cuenta](https://dle.rae.es/cuenta) support the selected senses and complements. The mandatory salirle a cuenta family has narrower evidence: [Salgado Viveros (2015), *Estrategias familiares de vida en contextos de pobreza rural: El caso de una familia inmigrante*, p.117](https://repositorio.colmex.mx/downloads/02870w06z?locale=es) directly attests worthwhile commercial return in Tlayacapan, Morelos fieldwork. This is contextual Mexican attestation, not measured educated frequency. [Fundéu's 2010 consultation](https://www.fundeu.es/consulta/traer-cuenta-2169/) instead prefers tener/traer cuenta and suggests Catalan influence; it is not cited as endorsing salir a cuenta. Original production examples require their own editorial adjudication. Clothing extensions use DEM [revés](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/rev%C3%A9s)/[remangar](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/remangar) and DLE [remangar](https://dle.rae.es/remangar), rather than counting an already-taught basic llevar = wear again.

Attributed recognition uses actual local situations and Mexican-compatible replies/paraphrases. ASALE's [Diccionario de americanismos](https://www.asale.org/damer/) country/register entries distinguish Colombian parce/parcero, bacano/chévere, tinto, onces, trancón, buseta and sumercé, and River Plate che, laburo, remera and colectivo. These often have additional national distributions; attribution does not claim exclusivity. [Cepeda Ruiz's Bogotá study](https://signoslinguisticos.izt.uam.mx/index.php/SL/article/view/248) supports close-friend ustedeo, while DPD [usted](https://www.rae.es/dpd/usted) supplies third-person grammatical agreement; neither implies all Colombian address is uniform.

DPD [vos](https://www.rae.es/dpd/vos)/[voseo](https://www.rae.es/dpd/voseo) support the selected Buenos Aires familiar forms, retained te/tu and ordinary accentuation, including unaccented sos and decime. [Recién](https://www.rae.es/dpd/reci%C3%A9n) with finite verbs is shared across much of America, including Mexico. [Borzi's institutional research record](https://bicyt.conicet.gov.ar/fichas/produccion/en/12015801) distinguishes dale acceptance/agreement from encouragement and reports broader country distribution. The original River Plate exchanges do not introduce an unmarked productive vos standard.

DLE [curro](https://dle.rae.es/curro), [ordenador](https://dle.rae.es/ordenador), [móvil](https://dle.rae.es/m%C3%B3vil), [piso](https://dle.rae.es/piso) and [zumo](https://dle.rae.es/zumo) support the selected Madrid lexical senses. NGLE [16.16](https://www.rae.es/gram%C3%A1tica/sintaxis/las-formas-de-tratamiento-ii-sustantivos-y-grupos-nominales) distinguishes familiar tío from literal uncle. DLE [valer](https://dle.rae.es/valer) and [Cestero Mancera/Moreno Fernández's Madrid study](https://ve.scielo.org/scielo.php?pid=S0798-97092008000100003&script=sci_arttext) distinguish agreement, encouragement and informal closing acts without invented frequency claims. DEM [coger](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/coger) and DLE [coger](https://dle.rae.es/coger) do not support an always-vulgar-in-Mexico rule. DPD [seseo](https://www.rae.es/dpd/seseo) recognizes cultivated American/Canarian/Andalusian usage; an individual Madrid speaker's written explanation of distinción is not a claim about all Spain or evidence of listening.

For 0976's attributed Madrid familiar-plural message, [DLE preferir, conjugation](https://dle.rae.es/preferir) distinguishes vosotros/vosotras preferís from ustedes/ellos/ellas prefieren, and [DPD vosotros](https://www.rae.es/dpd/vosotros) supplies the corresponding second-person plural agreement. The first quotation uses preferís consistently with decídmelo; the later Díganmelo paraphrase remains Mexican-compatible. This targeted agreement decision follows the [correction review](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/pull/16#pullrequestreview-5554457441) without generalizing one address pattern to all Spain.

DPD [quienquiera](https://www.rae.es/dpd/quienquiera), [cuanto](https://www.rae.es/dpd/cuanto) and [cual](https://www.rae.es/dpd/cual) support the selected indefinite/free, anaphoric and partitive relatives: quienquiera is written together and normally retains following que; nominal cuanto = all that is distinct from its determiner/adverb roles; whole-clause antecedents use neuter lo cual, and partitive dos de los cuales retains the article. These bounded authored examples do not silently introduce productive past subjunctive or a full later formal-relative system.

DPD [leísmo §4(f)](https://www.rae.es/dpd/le%C3%ADsmo) documents le/les morphology functioning as a personal direct object after impersonal se, especially masculine; accusatives also occur. DPD [ayudar](https://www.rae.es/dpd/ayudar) documents both transitive-direct and retained dative constructions in Mexico. Actual grammatical role follows the selected construction, not the clitic's spelling or personal a alone. These new se-related questions leave the accepted 02-0750 avisar-de decision unchanged.

DEM [por](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/por)/[para](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/para) distinguish attribution, pending action, purpose-less repeated infinitives, comparative circumstances and consequences alongside familiar cause/purpose relations. [Dar](https://dle.rae.es/dar) supports sufficiency and presumed states; [adentro](https://dle.rae.es/adentro) identifies the plural noun adentros for inner thought. DEM [responder](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/responder) supports responsibility through por, and [juzgar](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/juzgar) directly supports a juzgar por. These decisions support the selected complements, without making every occurrence of por/para a new retrieved rule or treating all complements of a verb as uniform.

DPD [cuórum](https://www.rae.es/dpd/cu%C3%B3rum) requires the accented Spanish adaptation cuórum; semiadapted quórum is rejected. The authored club example states its own attendance minimum and makes no legal-standard claim.

For budget inclusion, [DEM presupuestar](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/presupuestar) distinguishes making a budget or including an amount in one; [DLE presupuestar](https://dle.rae.es/presupuestar) also supplies the participle. The later budget objective therefore adds a specific financial sense beyond the earlier resource-reservation verb.

The brand-new collocation nuevo de paquete has written Mexican attestation in [Radio UNAM’s Al aire content text](https://www.radiopodcast.unam.mx/podcast/audio/21937); only that page text was consulted. DEM/DLE paquete does not supply that exact sense, so it is not attributed to those dictionary entries. The original household example makes no claim of exclusive usage or frequency.

For the social-repair vocabulary, [DEM refacción](https://dem.colmex.mx/ver/refacci%C3%B3n) supports a machine replacement part. [RAE/ASALE GTG, scheme 22, p. 362](https://www.rae.es/gtg/docs/GTG_esquemas_web.pdf) identifies mejor dicho as a reformulating or corrective discourse connector, distinguished here from restating the same content and merely refining an approximate quantity.

[DLE majo](https://dle.rae.es/majo) supports colloquial praise of a pleasant person;
it adds vocabulary beyond the accepted casual greeting. For the appointment
frame, [DLE quedar §8](https://dle.rae.es/quedar) supplies arranging a meeting,
while [DPD quedar §1(c)](https://www.rae.es/dpd/quedar) retains the accepted
Mexican de-infinitive agreement frame. Neither is represented as an exact listing
of appointment con; the original person-complement example receives its own
editorial adjudication. Regional perfect support is distinguished from its new
hidden complement.

## Complete §6.4 reconciliation

The following ranges identify actual learning-bearing targets and contexts. The
per-card table gives the precise hidden unit, kind and support. No visible word
alone establishes completion of a family or a second target.

| Required §6.4 inventory | Actual ranges (es-03-) | Learning-bearing distinctions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Indefinido versus imperfecto | 0001–0130; 0141–0150 | Event versus background, repetition and bounded duration; connected narrative senses and explicitly timed changes. |
| Regional perfecto | 0131–0140 | Attributed Madrid completed-event/result reports and Mexican-compatible retellings; new lexical components are distinguished from directly retrieved tense choices. |
| Future and conditional | 0151–0225 | Prediction, commitment, current/past conjecture, concession, supported hypotheses and future relative to an earlier report. |
| Present subjunctive foundations | 0226–0290 | Wish, request, evaluation, factive emotional reaction, desired referent, prospective temporal relation and actual assertion/intention contrasts. |
| Commands with pronouns | 0291–0360; 0401–0430 | Whole attached command forms, recoverable objects/recipients, negative placement, respectful address and speaker-inclusive exhortations. |
| Complete direct/indirect order | 0291–0400; 0405–0420 | Ordered clitic blocks and actual participants; ethical/affected roles, permitted versus blocked climbing, gerund attachment and written command spelling. |
| Passive and impersonal se | 0431–0500; 0541–0560 | Patient agreement versus singular impersonal agency, human-object case and selected lexical/pronominal/other-se contrasts. |
| Accidental constructions | 0501–0540; 0541–0560 | Affected-person mishaps, forgotten subjects and number agreement, recoverable possessions and distinctions from other se roles. |
| Relative clauses | 0561–0590 | Free, partitive, anaphoric, source and participant relations with supported complements and actual hidden links. |
| Reported speech | 0591–0620 | Attributed assertions, source stance, information questions, deixis and reported commitments without silently adding past subjunctive. |
| Cause, purpose, consequence, opposition | 0621–0660 | Recoverable reasons, intended goals, result/conclusion, comparison, concession and correction; visible relations are support when a new lexical component is hidden. |
| Si clauses | 0637–0640; 0661–0680 | Current provisos and exceptions, open future/recurring conditions, optional offers, factual premises, contingencies and indirect-question versus conditional si. |
| Ser/estar with adjectives | 0681–0740 | Interpretation-changing adjective senses, experiencer versus cause, characteristic versus current construal, result/condition, governed complements and genuine lexical extensions. |
| Systematic por/para | 0741–0780 | Representation, sufficiency, attributed/presumed state, internal speech, copy format, budgeted purpose, duration, route, evidential/benchmark relations, readiness/inclination and discourse functions. |
| Frequent verbal periphrases | 0781–0820; 0861–0865 | Modal, onset, transition, eventual outcome, development, accumulated result, obligation, threshold, intention and change frames; ongoing duration, nonoccurrence and task-time complements. |

Named G-family reconciliation:

| Required G family | Actual hidden mapping | Meaningful extension and prerequisite boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Darse cuenta de que | 0821 de que; 0822 me doy; 0823 sin darme cuenta; 0824 date | Governed content link, realization, lack of awareness and a direct noticing appeal; no complete realized proposition is hidden. |
| Tener algo en cuenta | 0831 en cuenta; 0832 tómala | Considering a pending decision constraint or a person's contribution, with actual personal-object agreement. |
| Por cuenta propia | 0836–0837 por cuenta propia | Independent employment versus completing an ordinary task without others' help. |
| Salirle a cuenta a alguien | 0841 a cuenta | Worthwhile return with me beneficiary and the same journeys priced under both options; 0842 retrieves desembolso rather than a negative/person-only replay. The reference qualification above applies. |
| Llevar + duration + gerund | 0861 llevo; 0862 llevaba | Ongoing activity measured through now or a stated past event; accepted 02-0556 supplies bare elapsed time plus location, not this gerund complement. |
| Llevarse bien con | 0866 me llevo; 0867 mal | Interpersonal rapport versus strained relations despite continued cooperation; later distanciarse/tutearse and things' compatibility are separately mapped extensions. |
| Llevar a cabo | 0871 a cabo; 0873 se | Implementation versus physical transport, and event-subject passive realization versus active agent. The 0871 source states a plan; it does not claim completed cleaning. |
| Clothing llevar | 0876 al revés; 0897 remangada | Inside-out orientation and an agreeing rolled-sleeve garment-state complement beyond accepted 01-0283/02-0123 basic wearing and 02-0721 traer puesto. |

Ordinary/social breadth appears in 0901–0960: absence and shared company,
gratitude/apology, bounded compromise, accepted commitments, shared living,
explanation/correction, clothing and everyday objects, cultural events and
participation, practical help and personally meaningful evaluation.
Attributed recognition occupies 0961–0976 (Spain), 0977–0988 (Colombia) and
0989–1000 (River Plate), in addition to Madrid recognition in 0131–0140.
These are locally attributed utterances with compatible Mexican responses or
paraphrases, not claims that shared usage is exclusive. Pronunciation support
is written explanation rather than listening evidence.

The new lexical expansions cover ordinary family/friendship, shared meals,
household changes, neighborhood life and social plans alongside work/study,
travel/service and practical repairs. Speakers, listeners, sources, recipients,
affected people and event timing are supplied in the visible text; support does
not invent them. New complement/function learning is distinguished from earlier
01/02 basic uses, and shared regional usage is attributed without being labeled
exclusive. No later-stage, audio or listening requirement is claimed complete.

| es-03-0001 | A | Pensé with an infinitive introduces an intention formed in response to a completed incident, contrasting with the previously held plan expressed by pensaba esperar. | [§6.4](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/blob/e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f/languages/es.md#64-independent-communication) | A speaker explains to a peer how rain changed yesterday's transport plan; neutral Mexican Spanish. | P | es-01-0520 for pensar with an infinitive expressing intention; es-02-0126 for contrasting belief-related pensaba; es-02-0011 for empezar a; es-01-0294 for tomar transport. Introductory support: pensaba esperar = I was planning to wait; empezó a llover = it started raining; entonces = then; pensé tomar = I formed the intention to take; taxi = taxi; tomé = I took; lo refers to the taxi. | grammar | accepted | none |
| es-03-0002 | A | En voz alta supplies the new collocation pensar en voz alta, making a provisional thought during an incident an audible formulation rather than an unspoken belief. | [§6.4](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/blob/e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f/languages/es.md#64-independent-communication) | The speaker and Ana check a map and reject a proposed entrance after reading a staff-only sign; neutral Mexican Spanish. | P | es-02-0126 for pensaba introducing a belief; es-01-0087 for mapa; es-01-0585 for entrada; es-02-0013 for leyó. Introductory support: mientras = while; revisábamos = we were checking; pensar en voz alta = think aloud; creo que = I think that; luego = then; solo personal = staff only; buscamos = we looked for; otra replaces another puerta. | lexical | accepted | none |
| es-03-0003 | A | Creí introduces a bounded mistaken identification of a sound's source, promptly corrected by seeing who is at the door. | [§6.4](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/blob/e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f/languages/es.md#64-independent-communication) | A speaker recounts mistaking a delivery person's knocks for their brother's arrival; neutral Mexican Spanish. | P | es-02-0046 for creer in the preterite; es-02-0014 for a discrete hearing event; es-02-0031 for vi; es-01-0252 for puerta. | es-03-0004 | A | Creía expresses a previously held assumption extending through part of a past day before contrary evidence arrives, contrasting with a bounded mistaken reaction. | [§6.4](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/blob/e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f/languages/es.md#64-independent-communication) | A speaker explains a mistaken assumption about their sister having a key, corrected by finding it in the speaker's bag; neutral Mexican Spanish. | P | es-02-0046 for bounded preterite creer; es-02-0126 for an imperfect background belief; es-01-0561 for desayuno; es-01-0565 for bolsa; es-02-0052 for encontré. | es-03-0005 | A | Entendía under visible negation presents sustained lack of comprehension during an explanation, contrasting with later attainment expressed by entendí. | [§6.4](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/blob/e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f/languages/es.md#64-independent-communication) | A learner describes struggling with a teacher's explanation of two prices and then understanding through receipt comparison; neutral Mexican Spanish. | P | es-01-0020 for no entiendo; es-02-0051 for finally reaching understanding with entendí; es-02-0547 for durante; es-01-0556 for recibo. | es-03-0006 | A | Entendí with an explicit object and como complement means interpreting someone's remark in a particular way, beyond successfully comprehending an instruction. | [§6.4](https://github.com/25d-228/meiki-decks/blob/e1d8239b7560f26b092cc7bb97c005738b34361f/languages/es.md#64-independent-communication) | A speaker explains interpreting a female friend's response as acceptance; the friend explicitly corrects that interpretation and explains her earlier wording. | P | es-02-0051 for entendí meaning reaching comprehension; es-01-0229 for está bien accepting a proposal; es-02-0028 for decir introducing quoted words; es-02-0111 for quería expressing an ongoing wish; es-02-0139 for cambio.
